#7456. Meta expands content protection to Instagram

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company is expanding its Facebook content protection systems, which use automatic detection features and alert creators when people repost their content.

Meta is expanding its content protection tools to Instagram, which will alert creators when other users repost their content, and enable them to take action on IP violations.

Meta added content protection on Facebook last year, which enables creators to activate protections for their original posts. Meta’s system then scans uploads as they come in, and alerts users to any copies of their content.

Now, the feature is coming to Instagram, with eligible creators able to enroll and protect their future uploads.

As per Instagram: “For eligible creators who enroll, content protection is a tool that automatically looks out for visual matches of your original videos, alerts you when we find a match across Instagram and Facebook, and lets you decide what happens next.”

That could offer peace of mind for creators who often see their content copied by aggregators and spammers.

Though it will be interesting to see how the system handles artificial intelligence-generated copies, which have become a much bigger issue of late.

With generative AI, users can easily create a replicant, or re-skinned version, of an original video in a completely different setting, or with completely different characters. As highlighted by the BBC News, one of the most popular examples of this trend has seen users creating alternate versions of the “Hotel Lobby” video by musicians Quavo and Takeoff.

Last month, Instagram Chief Adam Mosseri said that many creators have raised this trend as a concern, and added that he was also “very worried” about it.

Though he said that taking action will be tricky.

As Mosseri posted on Threads: “We are actively working out what to do, it’s just a tricky problem. We already let people make copyright claims against content, and take stolen copyrighted material. That’s not enough though, as assume many comedians and dancers haven’t gone through the trouble to copyright their material. We need a way to help those creatives without letting bad actors make more fake claims (happens all the time unfortunately).”

Instagram is experimenting with the addition of transcript detection for this exact purpose, which will alert creators when it detects a video that matches the spoken words in their content, even if the visuals have been altered by AI.

Instagram said that it’s testing this element with a small group of creators.

AI replicants will likely become a bigger problem moving forward, as more viral videos are re-framed, re-cast and re-created. Maybe, through expanded reporting, and new tactics like transcript detection, as well as the resulting copyright claims from those processes, Meta will be able to gain more training data to enhance its detection systems.

Though it’s unclear how keen Meta will actually be keen to crack down on AI use, given that its business has gone all-in on AI.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

WordPress 7.1.5 patches XSS, SQL injection, information disclosure, and more, including one flaw that could lead to remote code execution.

WordPress announced the release of version 7.1.5, a security update that fixes 18 vulnerabilities plus two security improvements. The update addresses several types of vulnerabilities, including cross-site scripting and SQL injection, as well as an unauthenticated flaw that could lead to remote code execution.

WordPress 7.1.5 fixes 20 issues covering a range of vulnerabilities across WordPress core. One of the vulnerabilities in particular stands out because it could lead to remote code execution (RCE), a type of flaw that can result in a site takeover.

The first one that stands out is an unauthenticated privilege escalation vulnerability affecting the Template Part block attributes which could lead to remote code execution (RCE), a type of flaw that can result in a site takeover.  There is no authentication required to exploit this vulnerability, and it could lead to RCE. That combination is the worst-case scenario for a CMS.

A remote code execution (RCE) is a vulnerability that enables an attacker to run their own code on someone else’s server from somewhere else, usually over the internet. For a WordPress site, that means the attacker can make the web server execute commands of their choosing. From there they can read or change files and make entries to the database, create admin accounts, install backdoors, or inject malware.

Multisite Administrator to Super Admin takeover (Customizer menu previews). This vulnerability requires an Administrator account, but on a multisite installation that means that an attacker who achieves admin level credential on one site can attain control of the entire network of sites.

Unauthenticated stored XSS via shortcode attributes in comments. No login is needed to exploit it. Stored XSS is a type of flaw that can run malicious code in the browser of anyone who views the affected comment, including administrators, which can lead to account takeover.

WordPress urges site owners to update to version 7.1.5 immediately to address security vulnerabilities. The update is available through the WordPress dashboard or as a download, with automatic updates supported. Security fixes are also available for older affected WordPress versions, although only the latest version is actively supported.

#7454. Snapchat will show more info on mutual friends

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The app will allow users who opt in to see a list of friends they have in common with someone, directly on their profile.

Snapchat wants to get more people talking in the app, so it’s expanding its info display on mutual friends. The updated format will enable users to see expanded profile info for any friends that they have in common with connections.

As shown in these example screens, Snapchat’s mutual friends update will give users more info about friends they have in common directly on their profile display.

To be clear, Snapchat already displays a count of how many friends users have in common. The mutual friends feature builds on that by showing who those friends are.

That could get more people interacting, and given the slowdown of Snapchat’s audience growth, that could be a critical focus.

Snapchat’s user growth has been stagnating, especially in the U.S. and Europe, where its daily active user count has been flat through the third quarter. As such, Snapchat needs to capitalize on its existing audience in order to maximize opportunities and drive advertiser interest.

Maybe offering more specific connection tools could help boost audience interest.

The question then is whether Snapchat users will actually want to display their expanded information to friends of friends, or whether they’d prefer to keep their Snapchat friend circles exclusive.

Given the more private focus of the app, the latter seems more likely, but again, Snapchat needs to try new things in order to drive user activity.

Snapchat also said that both the user and their connections will have to opt-in to the feature before their expanded information is displayed to others.

Mutual friends is now available to Snapchat users in the U.S., Australia, France and Sweden on iOS and Android.

#7452. Holiday TV ads from retailers arrive earlier, more frequently in 2026

While retail historically commands the season, it accounted for 63.7% of holiday ads in October, pacing ahead of recent years.

The holidays are always a busy time for advertising, and Nielsen’s data show activity moving further up the calendar as brands try to make the most of a packed Q4. More campaigns are starting as early as September, with holiday creative this year accounting for 1% of all linear TV ads during the month. Even as ad-supported streaming expands, linear remains on a growth trajectory after surpassing $1 billion in spend in 2023, according to Nielsen.

Some elements of the holidays remain constant, including retail’s dominance, which is particularly pronounced so far this season. The category has commanded an average of 45% of linear TV ad spend over the past five years, and only a slightly smaller share of the burgeoning streaming environment. In October, 63.7% of holiday ads came from retailers.

Promotional messaging is a major theme, with over one-third of retail ads assessed by Nielsen over the past five years carrying mentions of Black Friday or Cyber Monday deals events. Retailers are currently under pressure to reach wary shoppers during a persistent run of inflation. Several campaigns have been themed around more thoughtful, personalized gifting instead of low prices alone.

Effective holiday strategies can boost performance, per Nielsen connected TV benchmark studies for 2025. Cosmetics brands recorded a 3.2 times sales lift compared to non-holiday campaigns while department stores experienced a 2.4 times sales lift, the measurement firm found. Restaurants and fast-food brands recorded sales lifts of 1.5 times and 1 times, respectively, versus non-holiday ads.

Outside of retail, category spending trends have fluctuated more noticeably over time. Automotive shifted from representing 16% of holiday linear TV ad spend in 2023 to just 7.7% in 2025. Nielsen attributed the retreat to a bigger focus on streaming, where auto makes up 18.4% of the market. A dearth of car commercials, once a fixture of prime time programming, has gained media attention as the industry navigates economic headwinds and the failure of electric vehicle bets to take off in the U.S.

Food and beverage advertising also dipped between 2024 and 2025, which Nielsen attributed to big brands tightening their budgets and challenger brands only running smaller experiments for the holidays. Large food and beverage marketers including Pepsi and Oreo have rolled out holiday efforts in the past few weeks, with the latter introducing its first new seasonal creative in five years.

#7450. Your WordPress Sitemap Isn’t Broken – Chrome 158 Has Dropped XSLT via @sejournal, @TaylorDanRW

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

Avoid unnecessary WordPress sitemap fixes when Chrome drops XSLT, and use Google Search Console to verify that raw XML still works for search crawlers.

On Nov. 17, Chrome 158 rolls out to the majority of desktop and mobile users and drops support for client-side XSLT (Extensible Stylesheet Language Transformations).

For years, WordPress core and major SEO plugins, including Yoast SEO, Rank Math, and All in One SEO (AIOSEO), have referenced an .xsl stylesheet inside their XML sitemaps. When a person opened a sitemap URL in a browser, Chrome executed that XSLT file to transform the underlying XML code into a clean, clickable HTML table.

Without XSLT support, opening a WordPress sitemap in Chrome 158 displays raw, unstyled XML markup alongside a browser console and page warning.

Search engines do not use XSLT. Googlebot, Bingbot, and other web crawlers fetch and parse the raw XML nodes (, ) at the source. Crawlers avoid browser-side stylesheets, meaning indexing, crawl efficiency, and search rankings remain unaffected.

Because Chrome 158 lands while Google’s November core update, which began on Nov. 10, is still rolling, site owners and clients checking their domains during routine ranking audits may panic when they see raw XML in place of the familiar formatted table.

Depending on your site configuration, WordPress serves its sitemap index from one of two primary URLs:

If you open either URL in Chrome 158 or a newer release, seeing a wall of XML tags confirms the browser skipped the XSLT stylesheet, not that the sitemap file is corrupt or broken.

To verify, or prove to a nervous client, that Google reads the sitemap without trouble, check Google Search Console:

As long as Search Console reports “Success,” Google parses the XML structure without error.

XML sitemaps exist for machine consumption, and search engine bots read the underlying and tags. Dropping visual styling changes nothing about URL discovery, crawl budget, or search visibility.

Because Chrome 158 arrives alongside the November core update, clients or stakeholders who inspect their sitemap URLs will spot unformatted XML code and assume a site outage or plugin failure caused ranking fluctuations.

Chrome logs a deprecation warning in DevTools whenever a page invokes an .xsl stylesheet. Automated site-health crawlers or QA tools that monitor browser console errors may flag sitemap URLs until plugins or WordPress core drop the stylesheet reference.

Editors and junior marketers who relied on opening /sitemap_index.xml in Chrome as a quick directory of site URLs lose that visual table unless they switch browsers (such as Firefox or Safari, which retain XSLT for the time being) or use a dedicated HTML sitemap.

Because search crawlers ignore XSLT, you don’t need to rush to make emergency fixes. Choose from the steps below based on your role and client needs:

Note: This is only a visual change. Chrome 158’s removal of XSLT support only affects how sitemaps render in human browsers. Search engine crawlers process raw XML directly and are completely unaffected.

Organic performance, indexing, and rankings remain safe. WordPress 7.2 will remove the stylesheet reference and, with it, the Chrome warnings, leaving core sitemaps as plain XML. Yoast SEO 28.9 will restore a styled view using CSS, while Rank Math and AIOSEO have not announced a change.

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform highlighted 20 global influencers for 2027, which could offer valuable partnership insights for brands.

#7448. Why Qualcomm teamed with F1’s new star in race for sports marketing ROI

The company’s consumer brand Snapdragon signed 20-year-old phenom Kimi Antonelli as a brand ambassador as it looks to connect with racing’s growing audience.

Snapdragon, the consumer brand of chipmaker Qualcomm, on Tuesday launched an anthem film around its partnership with Formula 1 driver Kimi Antonelli. The 20-year-old driver for the Mercedes-AMG Petronas team — of which the brand is also a partner — became the youngest Formula 1 World Champion ever earlier this month.

The video is part of a campaign timed to the 2026 Las Vegas Grand Prix on Nov. 21. It will run on F1 broadcasts outside the U.S. on Sky, on digital and in partnership with channels including Sports Illustrated and YouTube. Apple TV is the exclusive broadcast partner of F1 in the U.S., which prevents brands like Snapdragon from running traditional broadcast ads around the race.

“We've got other ways to bring it to life,” Qualcomm CMO Don McGuire said of the campaign. “We've got creators involved, we've got remixing involved, we have slipstreaming involved… hopefully turning it into an anthem for him that has really long legs.”

A 60-second hero video follows Antonelli during a day at a hotel. Wearing smart glasses and using a tablet, both powered by Snapdragon chips, the phenom listens to a version of the traditional Neapolitan song “Funiculì, Funiculà,” trains in the gym and kicks off a social media trend. The ad ends with a tagline, “Jamme, Jamme. To the top!,” which quotes the song and speaks to his meteoric rise in racing.

“We've been watching Kimi for quite some time, and we have always believed that he was meant for greatness and that he was going to ascend very quickly. I think we made the right bet,” McGuire said.

Snapdragon’s partnership with Antonelli builds on the brand’s deal with Mercedes-AMG Petronas, a strategic collaboration that kicked off in 2023. Qualcomm had previously partnered with the team in the 2010s, a deal that was not “ROI-driven, marketing-driven,” said McGuire, who became CMO in 2021. The new partnership looks to better connect the brand’s objectives with the growing audience of F1, both in the U.S. and globally.

“The sport more and more is not something that I can judge based on [advertising value equivalency]. We have some pretty good placements on the Mercedes cars, especially with cockpit cameras and things like that, but it's just so fleeting, from a visibility standpoint,” McGuire said.

Those issues are exacerbated by the Apple TV deal, which limits brands’ ability to measure impact during broadcasts. Qualcomm is committed to F1, Mercedes-AMG Petronas and Antonelli, especially as it seeks to bring the partnership from content and storytelling through activations and hospitality.

“We believe we're already seeing higher ROI coming out of some things that we've done in the short term,” McGuire said. “We believe in the sport, why we were there and the fundamentals.”

Outside of F1, Snapdragon is no stranger to sports. Since the start of the 2024-25 season, the brand has been the principal shirt sponsor of Manchester United. Despite the iconic soccer team’s on- and off-field issues in recent years, the deal has yielded “humongous ROI” for Snapdragon, McGuire said.

“When you're dealing with a sports brand that big, at scale, the brand can withstand the ebbs and flows of performance on the pitch because the fan base is so huge,” the executive explained. “That's the benchmark that we use [for sports partnerships] and maybe it's a little unfair because they are arguably the biggest sports brand in the world.”

Snapdragon’s latest F1 marketing comes on the heels of a holiday campaign that launched earlier this month. At the heart of “The Gift That Changes Everything” is a sweet 60-second hero spot that shows how a mom and dad make Christmas seem to come sooner. The parents use a Snapdragon-powered PC to learn how to build a "time machine" that gives their child a special Christmas morning experience.

The campaign is running across broadcast, connected TV and paid social in the U.S. with CTV and paid social in the U.K., Germany and France. Created in partnership with 72andSunny New York and 72andSunny Toronto, the effort iterates on a “Snapdragon. That’s How” creative platform that launched earlier this year. The campaign has seen strong preliminary results, McGuire reported.

The holiday creative arrives as the brand prepares for a season that could see hardware makers impacted by memory shortages, reducing Snapdragon’s total addressable market at a time when the Nvidia scandal is roiling the chip market. The brand continues to work to build awareness and affinity, regardless of short-term market conditions.

“This is about building muscle and emotion that can sustain the long-term,” McGuire said. “We feel really strongly that we had to lean into holiday as if it were any other holiday season, regardless of the macro conditions.”

#7447. LaunchDarkly: Director, People Operations

LaunchDarkly is looking for a Director, People Operations to lead the operating model, service delivery, and continuous improvement of People Operations and Talent Acquisition operations across the employee and candidate lifecycles.

#7446. Meta launches messaging app for teens

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The company’s new Direct app is designed to expand Instagram DMs to a separate platform, and is almost exactly the same as the Direct app that the company launched in 2017.

Meta is testing a new, separate direct messaging app, which aims to expand Instagram DMs to its own platform, and could help Meta soften the impacts of expanding teen social media bans.

Called Direct, this latest app is aimed at teenagers, and offers a range of creation tools and features that align with teen usage behaviors.

As shown in these example images, Direct users draw on their chat threads, view inbox notes — a feature ported from IG — add stickers and voice effects, and more.

None of which is overly surprising, because Meta has actually launched this exact app before.

Back in 2017, Meta launched a messaging app for younger users, which was also called "Direct," and looks very similar to this new iteration.

That version of Direct was also focused on fun DM interactions, and included elements like drawing and effects in order to appeal to younger audiences.

Which obviously didn’t catch on then, so why is Meta now treading the same path, and trying, once again, to facilitate DM interaction between younger users?

Well, primarily, because Snapchat is struggling.

Snapchat’s user growth has stalled in the U.S. and Europe, with the app’s daily active user counts actually declining in both markets throughout the year.

With interest in Snapchat waning, Meta likely sees an opportunity to step in and provide an alternate platform for teen DMs.

It’s also worth noting that Meta launched an initial test of Direct in Turkey, which just this month enacted restrictions on social media usage for users under age 15.

As reported by Politico, Turkey’s social media ban for users under age 15 came into effect on Nov. 1. The law relates to all foreign-originated social media apps, but messaging apps are not included in the restrictions.

As such, Meta likely sees an opportunity to step in and scoop up any teens who may get booted from Snapchat as a result of this update.

Of course, Instagram and Facebook will also be impacted by these restrictions, and providing an alternative option for teen connection could ensure that Meta can soften any potential usage impacts. But an alternative messaging app won’t necessarily reduce the impact on its ads business, as it’s unlikely to inject ads into a DM focused app.

This is why Snapchat is likely the bigger focus, in terms of diluting its market share and increasing Meta’s own dominance.

In February, Snapchat reported that it had locked or disabled over 415,000 user accounts in Australia that the company identified as belonging to users under age 16, in line with Australia’s teen social media restrictions.

Australia’s population is around a third of Turkey’s, which points to the potential opportunity if Meta can replace Snap. Meta is also testing Direct in Thailand as well, which is also considering tougher teen social media restrictions, according to Thai PBS World.

Meta could use this as an opportunity to become the next critical platform for teen user engagement, with the scale of its other apps helping to promote its direct messaging app to users who would be impacted by these bans.

Meta also said that Direct will include a range of advanced protection measures, and will be subject to Meta’s parental supervision tools.

It’s an interesting tactic, though it will likely raise the eyebrows of local officials, who could see this as an attempt by Meta to circumvent social media restrictions. However, the logic applied to teen social media bans, in terms of content exposure, doesn’t necessarily apply to messaging apps.

And if Meta has the scale and capability to quickly scale messaging platforms faster than its competitors, it makes sense. And given that it’s already launched the exact same app once before, the broader effort here is likely worth the test.

#7445. Maker’s Mark spotlights signature red wax top in visual refresh

The brand evokes its heritage and craftsmanship with a new color palette, website and typography.

Maker’s Mark, a Suntory Global Spirits brand, pulled back the curtain on a new global brand identity this week that updates the brand’s color palette, photography and graphic elements with a more minimal, modern aesthetic that evokes the brand’s heritage. It is live in the U.S., China, Australia and the U.K., with other markets planned throughout 2027, according to a press release.

The new visual identity spotlights the bourbon’s iconic red wax. Custom typography is inspired by the handwriting of Margie Samuels, one of the co-founders of Maker’s Mark, who died in 1985 and was responsible for creating the brand name as well as its original bottle design. The bottle design remains unchanged, per press materials. Tactile elements included in the wax seal, such as wheat stalks and oak rings, were created using materials found at Star Hill Farm, one of the properties owned by the brand and the namesake of its Star Hill Farm Whisky.

"In a world increasingly driven by speed and automation, we still believe some things are worth making slowly, by hand," said Chloe Lloyd-Jones, vice president of global brand marketing at Maker's Mark, in press materials.

The brand last made changes to its visual identity in 2021, but they were not as extensive. The latest refresh was developed in partnership with Turner Duckworth London and is part of the “Perfectly Unreasonable” campaign.

The new design will appear across digital, social and retail channels, along with a new website. The overhaul is supported by in-person experiences in the U.S. and China, along with out-of-home activations in Australia and the U.K.

The refresh comes as the bourbon industry faces significant challenges, including slowing demand and a bloated inventory, which are driving down prices. Tariffs have also taken a toll on the industry, with exports of American whiskey, which includes bourbon, falling 19% in 2025. The industry’s issues are exacerbated by the general trend of Americans drinking less overall. A little over half of Americans said they drink, according to a recent Gallup poll, the lowest it’s been since the organization started measuring the trend in 1939.

Suntory Global Spirits ceased production at its flagship Jim Beam distillery in Clermont, Kentucky at the beginning of 2026. It recently announced it would continue to pause distilling operations until at least the end of next year. Maker’s Mark is distilled at a different location, which remains operational.

“Perfectly Unreasonable” launched in 2025 and is the first campaign for the brand by Carmichael Lynch. In December, Maker’s Mark entered into its first sports league sponsorship and became the first official spirits partner of the women’s basketball league Unrivaled.

Suntory Beverage & Food Limited, which is traded on the Tokyo Stock Exchange, saw its revenue increase 9.8% year-over-year for the nine months ending Sept. 30, 2026, according to financial results.

#7444. Google’s Next Update Won’t Touch The Index, And Nobody Will Announce It via @sejournal, @DuaneForrester

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Google Research describes a model that folds what it learns into its weights on a cycle. The knowledge cutoff becomes a schedule you cannot see.

The viral version of Google’s latest research post says the model makes itself smarter, and the post itself says something more useful to anyone whose job depends on what Google remembers about their company. On Oct. 27, Ali Behrouz and Vahab Mirrokni of Google Research published the sleep framework, a method that gives a language model a scheduled offline phase in which it moves what it picked up during use into its long-term weights, then rehearses on data it generates for itself. The viral framing is wrong in a specific way: the post states that the method operates within a fixed architecture under human-defined training rules, so nothing is modifying itself without supervision. What that framing misses is the part that matters here. If this design ships, the knowledge inside Google’s models stops being frozen at a cutoff and starts updating on a cycle that nobody outside Google will see.

In March I argued that the training data cutoff had become a ranking factor, because content on the two sides of the cutoff lives in different systems with different confidence behavior, and I suggested timing important claims to land before major training runs. That advice assumed the cutoff was a wall with a date on it. This paper describes a wall that moves on its own schedule, without a date, and the right way to read it is as the next form of the Google update: one that changes what the model believes rather than how the index is ranked, and that arrives with no announcement because there is no event to announce.

The post describes a two-phase lifecycle. During a wake phase, the model takes input and holds new information in fast, short-term memory components, which is roughly what context does today. During a sleep phase, it stops taking input and runs two stages. The first is consolidation. The model unlocks new, previously dormant low-rank experts inside a mixture-of-experts layout, assigns them to slower-updating memory blocks, and distills the short-term traces into them through a process the authors call knowledge seeding, which in plain terms means the new facts get their own fresh parameters rather than being written over old ones. Then the temporary parameters are reset so the capacity can be reused. The second stage is what the authors call dreaming. The model generates synthetic samples by randomly routing across its experts so that knowledge from different domains mixes, filters those samples with a gradient-based selection method, and fine-tunes on the ones that survive.

The reported results come from four settings: sequential translation into languages the model had never seen, where standard fine-tuning and in-context approaches forgot earlier languages, and the sleep model held a near-zero performance drop; recall on a long-context benchmark out to ten million tokens; factual knowledge incorporation from document passages, where the sleep model beat supervised fine-tuning and a prior self-editing method; and math reasoning on an open 8-billion-parameter base model. The underlying paper has been on arXiv since June and on OpenReview since September 2025, so the blog post is Google deciding to say this out loud, not Google discovering it.

This is not a one-off. The same authors published Nested Learning in November 2025 with the Hope architecture and the idea of a continuum memory system, a chain of memory blocks that update at different frequencies, with the fast blocks holding short-term information and the slow blocks holding durable knowledge. The sleep framework is what you build once you have blocks that update at different rates: a schedule for moving information from the fast ones to the slow ones. Titans came before Hope, Hope came before sleep, and the arc now runs about two years. The autoregressive ranking paper I covered in September closed a five-year arc on how results get ordered. This one is younger and just as deliberate.

It is research, not a product. The post makes no statement about deployment, timelines, or which Google models might use it. The experiments run on open Qwen models at 1.7 and 8 billion parameters, not on Gemini. No compute cost is reported for a sleep cycle, and no one outside the team has replicated the results. In the experiments, the new facts arrive as documents, not as user conversations or crawled web pages, so anyone claiming the model learns from your chats is extrapolating past the paper.

Those caveats are real and they do not change things. Google publishes research arcs and then ships pieces of them, and that pattern has held often enough that the practitioner question is not whether this exact method lands but what the design tells you about the direction. The direction is a model whose long-term memory is rewritten on a cycle.

Anyone who has done this work for more than a few years lived through the first version of this shift. Google says it updates Search thousands of times a year and shares only the ones it considers notable, and in a 2022 interview Danny Sullivan put the previous year’s experiments and quality tests above 800,000. The named core updates are the handful that get a dashboard entry. Everything else lands continuously and silently, and the industry adapted by building a diagnostic ritual around the named few: traffic moves, you check the date against the update trackers, and attribution follows.

Retrieval already took the same path for freshness. AI Overviews and grounded answers pull new pages daily with nothing announced, so a claim that silence is new would be wrong. What the sleep framework changes is which layer goes silent. Retrieval changes what the model can look up. Consolidation changes what the model believes without looking anything up. Those are different things for a brand. A retrieved fact is cited, hedged, and replaceable the next time the page is fetched. A consolidated fact is spoken with the confidence of something the model knows, and the March piece covered why that confidence difference shapes how a brand appears in an answer.

So the shape practitioners already know, announced core updates on the outside and unannounced tweaks between them, gets a second copy one layer down: announced model versions on the outside, unannounced consolidation accumulating between them. The ranking stack does not go away and this does not replace it. It sits on top of it, in the model that writes the answer.

The detail in the post I keep returning to is that consolidation is selective. The authors say the model is not asked to copy every detail from its short-term traces into its parameters, and the dreaming stage scores its own samples with a gradient-based filter before anything is trained on. That is a ranking function. It decides which facts the model will carry forward as durable knowledge and which it will let expire with the temporary parameters. Being seen during wake is not being learned. Being learned is what being indexed used to mean, and the thing deciding it is a loss calculation nobody outside Google can inspect.

Three consequences follow, and the first two are my inference from the design rather than claims the paper makes.

First, the filter will favor facts that are consistent across sources. A gradient-based selection method keeps samples that reduce error broadly, and a fact that agrees with everything else the model has seen reduces error more reliably than one that contradicts a competitor’s page, a stale directory listing, or your own older content. Conflicting versions of your company are no longer only a conflation risk at retrieval time, which I covered when German courts started holding the platform responsible for false answers, but a consolidation risk, because the version that gets learned is the one that fits best, not the one you prefer.

Second, dreaming means the model rehearses on data you never published. The synthetic samples are generated by mixing experts across domains, so the model’s representation of a company can shift through rehearsal with no new input from anyone. No competitor moved, no page changed, no update ran, and the answer about you is different anyway. This is a candidate mechanism for a question I have been asked repeatedly this year, which is why an answer changed when nothing observable changed.

Third, and this one sits in the paper’s design directly, there is a window. Between a fact entering the fast memory and the next consolidation, a correction is cheap because nothing durable has been written yet. After consolidation, fixing a false fact is no longer a matter of updating a page and waiting for a recrawl. It is unlearning, and removing one specific fact from a model’s weights without damaging the rest remains an open research problem. The term I would use is the consolidation window, the period in which a wrong fact can still be caught before the model commits it, and the uncomfortable property of the window is that its length and timing are unknown to you.

If no Google model announcement within roughly 18 months references periodic updates between releases, continual learning in production, or anything resembling a consolidation cycle, this stays a research direction, and the cutoff stays a wall. If the compute cost of a sleep cycle turns out to be comparable to a training run, the economics kill the cadence idea even if the method works. And if Google chooses to publish consolidation events the way it publishes core updates, the silence argument fails and the diagnostic ritual survives with a new row on the dashboard. I think the third is the least likely of the three, because Google has never published its unnamed changes and has said plainly that it does not intend to.

The useful response is to stop building process around an event that may stop existing.

The cutoff-aware calendaring I suggested in March still holds for the next major training run, because those will keep happening. What changes is the half-life of the word parametric. The safest assumption is that what the model knows about you is being revised on a schedule you cannot see, by a filter you cannot inspect, and that the only observable is the answer itself.

#7443. Snapchat shares insight into luxury shopping trends

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

A new study commissioned by the app found that 84% of its users said social media platforms made them feel close to the luxury universe.

Snapchat has published a new report which examines key trends among luxury shoppers, as well as the role that Snapchat itself plays in connecting shoppers to new luxury products.

The report is based on a survey of more than 9,000 luxury consumers across nine global markets, which was conducted by APP Media and Ifop group.

The data underlines the role that social media plays in raising awareness of luxury products, and specifically highlights Snapchat’s role in helping to inform and validate luxury shopping decisions.

The data shows that 76% of luxury shoppers agreed that social platforms make them feel close to the luxury universe. Snapchat users are even more aligned with this, with 84% of them agreeing with the statement.

The report also looked at how luxury shoppers approach buying, including their motivations for shopping for higher-end products.

The report also includes a range of helpful data points for marketing plans, such as info on the impact of scarcity.

In addition, the report also explored the role that peer validation plays in the luxury goods purchase process.

The latter is an important note for Snapchat specifically, because users in the app generally engaged with smaller groups of close friends within their message streams.

Indeed, the survey data showed that content from close connections is the key driver of luxury purchase decisions for Snapchatters.

The report also included notes on the kinds of content luxury shoppers respond to, which could help to guide strategic marketing approaches.

These are some interesting and potentially valuable notes for luxury brands, offering specific insights into how social platforms, and Snapchat specifically, can help to amplify messaging and drive purchase intent.

#7441. 21 Alternative Search Engines You Can Use Instead of Google via @sejournal, @lorenbaker

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

For many people, searching the web means opening Google. It’s a familiar place to research a purchase, check a fact, or catch up on the news and that reach shows in the numbers with Google accounting for 91.04% of worldwide search engine referrals according to Statcounter in October 2026.

But the search engine you use out of habit is not always the best tool for the job. Think about the last time you needed an image you could reuse, a webpage that had disappeared, or a more private way to search. Those are different problems, and a single search box is not always the easiest way to solve them.

You don’t have to give up Google to find out what you might be missing. We collated 21 alternative search engines and tools worth trying, whether you’re looking for a better answer or a new way for customers to find your business.

About the Usage Figures: Entries are ordered within each section by estimated monthly visits to the named website or subdomain. The October 2026 figures are not unique users, search queries, or app activity. Statcounter percentages shown separately measure search referral share, not visits.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 6 billion to chatgpt.com, October 2026. This covers the ChatGPT website, not Search alone.

ChatGPT is useful when your question needs a conversation rather than a fresh set of keywords each time. You can ask it to compare two approaches, explain an unfamiliar term, and then look for recent evidence without starting over. Its advantage is keeping the context of that investigation together.

OpenAI’s search documentation says it can retrieve current web information and link to sources. Search is available on free and paid plans, including for people who are not signed in, although usage limits apply. ChatGPT may search automatically, or you can select Search from its tools.

Use it to work through a question, then open the citations to check the answer. It can misread a source or miss a qualification, and its use of other search providers means it is not simply a separate, independent web index.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 88.3 million to perplexity.ai, October 2026.

Perplexity puts source-backed answers at the center of the search experience. Its research-oriented approach is useful when you want an overview of a subject and a trail of sources to investigate, rather than opening a long list of results before finding the main points.

Its Pro Search process can conduct multiple searches and bring findings together, with follow-up questions keeping the research in context. Depending on your plan, you can narrow the work to areas such as academic or financial sources. Access and limits vary by tier.

The benefit is a faster route into a topic, not a substitute for reading the evidence. Check that each citation supports the statement beside it because AI answers can still contain hallucinations even when links are present.

Why SEO Experts Should Care: Perplexity can be useful even if it sends less traffic than Google. In his post on why marketers should care about Perplexity, Dennis Goedegebuure argues that it is a low-cost test bench for AI search. Try the research questions your customers ask, inspect which pages get cited, and check how your brand and recently updated facts appear. For B2B marketers, that means testing visibility during product research, rather than judging the opportunity solely by overall visit totals.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 3.7 billion to bing.com, October 2026.

Bing is a full-service alternative for everyday web, image, video, and news searches. One concrete reason to try it is Visual Search: when you have a picture of a product, plant, or object but do not know its name, you can start with the image instead of guessing keywords.

Microsoft’s visual search tools can find related images and shopping options, identify objects, and extract or translate text from a picture. Conventional results remain available alongside AI-assisted tools, so an answer does not have to be the end of your search.

Search Engine Journal covered Bing’s search updates in December 2024, including work on faster, more precise results. Try the same image or video query on Bing and Google to see which makes the next step easier for you; that is more useful than assuming one wins every search.

Where available, Bing’s Copilot Search adds AI-generated summaries with source citations and suggested follow-up topics, so you can explore a question without starting from scratch.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 751 million to search.yahoo.com, October 2026.

Search Engine Referral Share: 2.54% in the U.S., October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Yahoo Search makes the most sense if you already use Yahoo for news, finance, or other daily information. Its value is the connection between search and that wider content ecosystem, rather than a completely different index of the web.

Yahoo introduced Scout in January 2026 as an AI answer engine combining open-web information with Yahoo content and data. The U.S. beta launch named Anthropic’s Claude and Microsoft’s Bing grounding API as parts of the system.

That gives Yahoo users a way to move from a headline or financial topic into a sourced explanation. You can still browse traditional search results, and you should follow Scout’s source links when an answer matters. The launch’s audience figures cover Yahoo’s broader services, not people using Scout alone.

At Search Engine Journal, Yahoo accounts for about 0.2% of our traffic. It’s a small share, but it still brings a few thousand visits in a month and for a different business, even that smaller stream of visitors could include a potential customer or lead to a sale.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 1.7 billion to baidu.com, October 2026. This includes non-search activity on the named website.

Search Engine Referral Share: 49% in China, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Baidu matters when your research or marketing is aimed at Chinese-language audiences. Its value is not simply in translating an English query, but it offers a view of Chinese-language search and a connected set of local content services.

Alongside web results, its homepage links to maps, images, video, the Baike encyclopedia, Zhidao questions and answers, and Wenku documents. Those services give you distinct places to investigate how a topic, product, or brand is described. Use the terminology that local sources use rather than assuming an English search will surface the same material.

For marketers, the search referral share is a reason to investigate Baidu separately, not proof that it will send a particular site traffic. Distinguish content hosted inside Baidu’s services from results linking to external publishers, and assess the original source.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 1.5 billion to naver.com, October 2026. This includes non-search activity on the named website.

Search Engine Referral Share: 42.25% in South Korea, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Naver is useful for Korean-language research because its search experience brings together more than external webpages. Naver’s search overview describes results across news, maps, cafés, blogs, images, video, and other categories.

That mix gives you a route into local experiences and community content as well as conventional reporting. Its community services include Naver Blog, interest-based Café communities, and Knowledge iN questions and answers. If you are researching a Korean product or audience, those are different sources to explore from an English-language web-results page.

For website owners, Naver Search Advisor is the official starting point for webmaster tools. Keep visits to an external website separate from engagement with a blog or other content hosted inside Naver.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 162.2 million to yandex.com, October 2026. This covers the international website, not total use across all Yandex domains.

Search Engine Referral Share: 70.22% in Russia, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Yandex is worth checking when you need Russian-language sources or are researching visibility in Russia. Its large share of measured referrals there gives it a different role from a small global alternative – it is an important regional search environment in its own right.

Use it to compare which local publishers and pages appear for the terminology your audience actually uses. Its international homepage also presents an AI search option, but the practical reason to investigate it is the regional results and audience context, not simply another chatbot interface.

SEJ’s Yandex guide provides background for marketers. The 2023 data leak is a separate historical event, not a verified description of today’s ranking system. The search referral share above describes referrals, not the percentage of Russian people using Yandex.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 16.5 million to sogou.com, October 2026.

Sogou offers another route into Chinese-language information, with a more specific reason to try it rather than simply comparing general web results – its WeChat search is a destination for discovering public accounts and articles from that ecosystem.

That is useful when the material you need was published as a WeChat article rather than an ordinary standalone webpage. Search in the relevant Chinese terminology and distinguish those public articles from results in Sogou’s general web search.

This is not access to private messages or every piece of content in WeChat. As with other platform-specific searches, check the account, publication date, and original article before using a result as evidence.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 698.8 million to search.brave.com, October 2026.

Brave Search combines an independent web index with tools for changing how results are ranked. It is worth trying if you want both an alternative to Google or Bing’s underlying results and more say over which sources rise to the top.

Its search help describes Goggles, which apply alternative ranking or filtering rules. Brave also describes domain-level ranking controls and an AI answer experience on its about page. Those controls give you something more specific to experiment with than a different-looking results page.

SEJ reported Brave’s Bing separation in 2023. You can use Brave Search in another browser, so testing its index does not require adopting Brave’s browser, wallet, VPN, or other products.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 681.9 million to duckduckgo.com, October 2026.

DuckDuckGo is a straightforward choice if you want everyday search without a personal search-history profile. You can use its search website in your existing browser, so the switch does not require changing your whole browsing setup.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 57.6 million to startpage.com, October 2026.

Its partner explanation identifies both Google and Microsoft Bing. Startpage says it does not share personal information with them, and that those providers see Startpage rather than the person making the request.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 4.6 million to kagi.com, October 2026.

Its Lenses let you narrow searches to selected websites or categories such as forums, academic sources, or PDFs. Kagi’s source documentation describes its own indexes alongside major and specialist providers, with an emphasis on surfacing independent and noncommercial content. It is not powered solely by its Teclis index.

As of November 2026, its monthly individual plans include Starter at $5 plus tax for 300 searches and Professional at $10 plus tax for unlimited searches. Annual billing has different terms. If you search heavily and want persistent control over sources, those features are the reason to consider paying.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 219 million to ecosia.org, October 2026.

Ecosia lets you put the revenue from an everyday habit toward climate work. It says all of its profits go to climate action, with the majority funding tree-planting projects. That is its central reason to exist: changing who benefits financially from search.

The company publishes monthly financial reports, so you can look beyond the tree counter to see how money is spent. Its provider documentation lists Bing, Google, and European Search Perspective, or EUSP; the mix depends on factors including region, device, and permissions.

This makes Ecosia a funding-model choice, not a promise that every query uses a wholly separate index. If you want to support its mission while keeping familiar web search, it is a low-friction option to try. You can check the provider through the information control beside a result.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 451,800 to karmasearch.org, October 2026.

KARMA applies the search-funded nonprofit model to animal welfare and biodiversity. Its distinction from a broader climate-focused service is the cause: helping fund organizations working on wildlife, habitats, and animal protection.

The company’s explanation says sponsored search links generate revenue for its nonprofit partners. Its site lists partners including Re:wild, Humane World for Animals, and iNaturalist. You can inspect those partnerships to decide whether they match the work you want to support.

Availability varies by country. During this review, a U.S. capture showed the search homepage, while a visit from another region displayed a notice that KARMA was unavailable there and would redirect searches to Bing. Check local availability before making it your default; a regional restriction does not mean the service has closed.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 155,800 to yep.com, October 2026.

Yep is an Ahrefs-built alternative with a distinctive audience beyond people typing into a search box: developers who want web results for an AI agent or research application. Ahrefs’ current Yep product page describes a first-party crawler, index, and ranking system, with result excerpts and filters for the information an application retrieves.

The public search interface captured for this review also returned conventional web results. For an everyday user, that makes it another index to compare; for a developer, the API is the clearer reason to investigate it. The current homepage emphasizes that developer-facing use rather than presenting a Google-like consumer pitch.

SEJ’s 2022 Yep interview explains the original proposal to share advertising revenue with creators. Although, the current homepage does not establish that those proposed payouts are operating, so they should not be the reason you choose it today.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 4.4 billion to x.com, October 2026. This covers platform website activity, not searches or app usage.

x.com is useful when the thing you need is a public statement or conversation: what an organization announced, what a named person said, or how a developing event is being discussed. You are searching posts on the platform, not a general index of webpages.

That direct access to posted statements is its advantage for news monitoring and research. Its advanced search is a further option for signed-in users; the public Explore route redirected to a sign-in screen during this review.

Use posts to locate leads and original statements, not as automatic confirmation of a claim. Check the account, timestamp, and supporting evidence, especially when an old post is circulating as if it describes a new event.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 2.1 billion to linkedin.com, October 2026. This covers platform website activity, not searches or app usage.

LinkedIn is useful when your search starts with a professional identity, such as a person, employer, role, or job. It can also help you find practitioners discussing a work-related subject, rather than only the publishers ranking for that subject on the open web.

The advantage is the professional context. A name alongside an employer and career history gives you a way to distinguish people who share a name and identify a relevant source. Its public people search is one starting point, although fuller profiles and other features can require signing in.

Treat profiles as self-reported information, not a verified company directory. Check current roles and dates against the person’s or organization’s own website when those details matter to your research.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 46.4 million to web.archive.org, October 2026.

The Internet Archive’s Wayback Machine answers a question a current search result often cannot: What did this webpage say before it changed or disappeared? It is useful for checking an old product claim, recovering a removed reference, or following the history of a website.

Enter the URL and inspect the available captures. The advantage is the dated record where you can compare versions rather than relying on a current page to explain its own past. Check the capture date and whether images and other resources were preserved.

The Wayback Machine is part of the wider Internet Archive, whose books, recordings, software, and other collections are separate resources. It is not a complete archive of every page, and a captured claim is evidence of what a page said then, not proof that the claim was true.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 39.3 million to slideshare.net, October 2026.

SlideShare is worth trying when you want the presentation itself, not another article summarizing the subject. A conference talk, research deck, or business pitch can reveal how someone structures an argument and illustrates the details.

The current homepage describes a collection of more than 25 million presentations. Browsing, reading, and uploading do not require a subscription, while features such as downloading presentations are paid benefits. The value is a format-specific collection you can explore for research or presentation inspiration.

Check the uploader and publication date before relying on a deck’s statistics or recommendations. Being able to view a presentation also does not automatically give you permission to reuse its artwork.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 3.1 million to wolframalpha.com, October 2026.

WolframAlpha is for questions that need computation rather than a list of pages. If you want to solve an equation, convert units, or work with structured facts, it can calculate an answer from its data and algorithms instead of leaving you to find a suitable calculator elsewhere.

Its explanation of the service describes dynamic computation over curated knowledge. The mathematics examples show the inputs it understands; other areas include science, dates, and measures.

That is a different job from comparing recent reporting or finding an opinion. Use it when the result should be computed, and check how it interpreted your units, assumptions, and wording before relying on the output.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 296,200 to openverse.org, October 2026.

Openverse is the tool to try when finding a usable image or audio track matters more than finding a webpage about one. It focuses on openly licensed and public-domain media, making it useful for an article, presentation, or other creative project.

Its About page describes a collection of more than 800 million images and audio tracks gathered from multiple repositories, with features such as one-click attribution. That collection size is separate from its website traffic. Its advantage is bringing reuse-oriented discovery into one place, rather than treating every image on the web as equally available to use.

Openly licensed does not mean free of conditions. Licenses can require attribution or restrict commercial use, and Openverse does not verify every work’s licensing information. Follow through to the original source and confirm the license before using a file.

The introduction of ChatGPT helped bring conversational interfaces into everyday use. In January 2023, SEJ reported that Google’s founders had responded by returning to discussions about its AI response and then opened access to Bard in March 2023.

Google rolled out AI Overviews to U.S. users in May 2024 and announced AI Mode in 2025. Those developments explain why many services in this list now combine web results with AI generated answers. The two formats can help with different parts of the same research task.

Google may be the most familiar option, but the best choice for a particular task can be different. Try a few real queries on two or three services, compare the sources, the ease of finding the original information, and the controls you will actually use.

You can keep one engine for everyday searches and use another for a specific job. A calculation, an archived webpage, and a professional profile do not need to start in the same search box.

Open your browser’s search settings and choose a supported default engine. If the service is not listed, use its official browser setup instructions. You can also visit its website directly without changing your default.

Some do. Startpage identifies Google and Bing as partners. DuckDuckGo largely sources traditional links and images from Bing. Kagi combines its own indexes with several providers. Brave Search builds an independent web index.

No. DuckDuckGo says it is an independent company. A search provider relationship is different from ownership, and its traditional links and images largely come from Bing.

No search tool can guarantee a neutral view of everything online. Index coverage, ranking rules, filters, location, and the query itself affect what appears. Compare independent sources and more than one search engine when the differences matter.

#7440. LinkedIn adds another in-stream game

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The math-based puzzle, called “Sumbreak,” is part of the platform’s push to increase user retention and engagement.

LinkedIn has launched its ninth in-app game, with a new math-based game called "Sumbreak."

As shown in these images, "Sumbreak" gets players to use math in order to merge numbered tiles and eventually get to a single tile.

As explained by LinkedIn Head of Games Helen Smith: “Each time you merge matching numbers on the grid, they add together, bringing you one step closer to clearing the board. The challenge is figuring out how to combine the numbers so you're left with a single final tile.”

LinkedIn members will be able to challenge others to "Sumbreak," offering another means to prompt friendly rivalry in the app and drive more engagement using games instead of relying on professional connections.

In June, when the platform launched its eighth puzzle game, called Wend, Smith said that billions of puzzles had been solved in the app since games were first added in-stream in May 2024.

LinkedIn hasn’t shared specific numbers on how many of its users are playing its puzzle games, but in March 2025, the platform reported that “millions of professionals” were playing its in-stream games each day, and that 84% of users who play a game return to try again the next day.

The idea is that these in-stream games will increase user retention and engagement and improve LinkedIn’s usage stats while also driving more opportunities for conversation and connection.

On another front, LinkedIn added favorites to gaming leaderboards, making it easier to compare performance against chosen connections and rivals.

LinkedIn also added bonus puzzles to reward players who return every day, and made its games easier to find in its updated UI.

Integrating games into a professional connection site may still feel like an odd fit, but the data suggests that LinkedIn users are benefitting from the feature.

#7437. Paid media now plays largest role in brand-funded creator growth: report

While pumping paid dollars into creator content is the biggest piece of the category pie, using creators for production is the fastest growing, Madison & Wall found.

Madison & Wall’s research illustrates how creator marketing is evolving further away from the narrow #sponsored post model — what the advisory and consulting firm calls audience-led spend — into a multifaceted media engine that is core to consumer outreach. The advertiser-funded creator economy is forecast to grow 15% this year to $116 billion and reach $178 billion by 2030. It is on pace to grow at twice the rate of the total ad market as brands try to meet demands around creative content volume, authenticity and audience access.

“[Creators] have become an essential layer of the commercial advertising and media supply chain, serving simultaneously as media channels, creative production houses, commerce drivers, and performance engines,” the report reads.

Putting paid media dollars behind existing creator content that performs well has become the largest aspect of the advertiser-funded creator economy, providing reach and scalability without the TV commercial gloss that many consumers have begun to reject. Reliably achieving organic reach has also become less certain as platform algorithms change. Due to these factors, media-led creator spending is expected to reach $83 billion by 2030.

Marketers are not broadly expanding their budgets to support creator initiatives, but rather siphoning off resources previously allocated to channels like traditional media, digital search, paid social and programmatic. Madison & Wall emphasized that creator partnerships often employ multiple approaches, including combining audience-led and media-led spend, rather than focusing on one bucket. The study did not analyze consumer-funded creator income, meaning subscriptions, tips or other forms of revenue creators receive from their audiences.

While media-led spending is the biggest piece of the creator pie at the moment, asset-led spending, where brands pay creators to produce content, effectively using them as a content studio, is the fastest growing, currently representing 12% of the market. That helps explain the budgets being drawn from traditional creative production and could be a detriment to third-party creative agencies and production shops, which are also contending with the rise of artificial intelligence-generated content.

Those marketing services intermediaries, as well as talent managers and technology platforms, are retaining about 8% of advertiser-funded creator investments on a net basis as part of what Madison & Wall terms infrastructure spend. The rest of the market is made up of a miscellaneous segment that accounts for 7% of spend and encompasses creator engagements such as live events, barter arrangements and name, image and likeness deals.

The second part of Madison & Wall’s study will break down some of the growing pains the creator economy faces as it looks to sustain momentum, including fragmentation, pricing inconsistencies, contractual logjams, measurement shortcomings and questions of ownership over areas like data and intellectual property. That installment will also dive into creator fatigue, a mounting problem for marketers who have valued creators as a more approachable and trustworthy way to catch consumer attention.

#7436. Snapchat enables Lens creation via third-party AI tools

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The new Easy Lens capability enables users to build Lenses using their chatbot of choice.

Snapchat is looking to help creators use their favorite AI chatbots and tools to create Lens effects for the app, with the launch of its new Easy Lens CLI, which provides a simplified connective process to link third-party AI tools into its Lens Studio system.

As shown in this image, Snapchat’s Easy Lens CLI provides a connector to help creators build Lenses via conversational prompts using the AI chatbot of their choice.

Creators can tap on the CLI option on the EasyLens website, which will provide them with instructions on how to guide their chosen AI chatbot towards Snap’s Lens creation parameters.

From there, creators can instruct the chatbot to make whatever Lens they like, and the system will convert those ideas into relevant instructions and code.

The system expands on Snapchat’s existing Easy Lens process, which makes it easier for anyone to create a Lens effect using AI prompts. This new expansion will essentially bring that same functionality to external AI chatbots, utilizing their processing capacity to build Lens features.

As explained by Snapchat: “Easy Lens already makes it simple to create a Lens from a single prompt on web and mobile. Easy Lens CLI brings that same creation flow into the tools developers use every day, so their AI assistant can take on the technical work while they focus on the creative vision.”

Snapchat said the process will enable creators to build everything “from Web Lens games with leaderboards to camera Lenses and beauty effects.”

“Their AI assistants can generate assets, build and test each Lens, fix errors along the way and send new versions to their phone in minutes,” Snapchat said. “That means a full game can go from an idea to a published Lens in a single day, without writing any code.”

The company added that all lenses created through this process will go through the same content checks as any other Snap Lens.

CLI-created Lenses will also be eligible for monetization for enrolled creators under Snapchat’s Lens+ payout scheme. Snapchat said it awarded $1.8 million in payouts to Lens+ community creators last month.

Easy Lens CLI will run on Mac, Windows and Linux, and is also available as a plugin for Claude and Codex.

The platform is testing an updated version of its Snap Creator Network, as well as new Visited By listings on its Snap Map.

Snapchat announced several new creator discovery and monetization opportunities, including an updated creator marketplace, new location markers for where creators have visited on the Snap Map, and an advanced agency partner program.

Snapchat has also introduced a new way for physical stores and locations to benefit from creator content, with Visited By listings on the Snap Map.

As shown in these examples, Visited By will add markers to places where creators that the user follows have filmed content. The highlighted listings will also include links to the relevant posts.

This could help brands tap into influencer recommendations, while also providing more incentive for stores to collaborate with popular creators in order to increase their presence in the Snap Map display.

That will ensure that more agency providers are aware of the company’s evolving creator partnership efforts, and how to utilize its advanced discovery tools to guide brands towards the right creator partners.

#7434. How Disney helps brands interact and transact with holiday audiences

The entertainment giant’s holiday ad sales are pacing 20% ahead of the same point last year, with demand across the retail, financial services, CPG and technology sectors.

The 60th annual Country Music Association Awards, which aired last night on ABC and Disney+ and will stream tomorrow on Hulu, showcased Disney’s evolving advertising strategy in action. Chevrolet debuted new custom creative, partnered with musician and Chevy truck owner Zach Top for several in-show moments and put out social-first, behind-the-scenes content.

In addition, Bath & Body Works, a new partner to the awards show, was the first brand to be a presenting sponsor of a live integration on TikTok that captured behind-the-scenes content and was hosted by musician Lauren Alaina and creator Haley Baylee. Financial services company Ally made its CMA Awards debut, activating on in-show, social, streaming and linear channels, including content with personality Kristin Cavallari.

“We're looking at new ways beyond your traditional 30-second ad in order to be able to engage fans as well as drive new revenue opportunities,” said John Campbell, senior vice president for streaming, entertainment and multicultural solutions at Disney.

The CMA Awards arrived with just a week until Thanksgiving. The holiday period is in full swing, with the National Retail Federation forecasting holiday retail sales of more than $1 trillion. With a portfolio that spans film, TV, streaming, theme parks and consumer products, Disney reaches 54% of holiday gift-givers monthly, per the company’s research, providing advertisers with a large audience across channels.

For Disney, advertising bookings in streaming and linear across Halloween, Thanksgiving and the holidays overall are pacing 20% ahead of the same point last year, with demand across categories including retail, financial services, CPG and technology, the company said.

The holidays are now a yearlong concern, with Disney selling its first holiday deal in January and more than 10% of its holiday revenue coming in since Nov. 1. The company also saw 10% year-over-year advertising revenue growth in Q4 of its fiscal year 2026 for a total of $1.69 billion. Across the advertising landscape, holiday ads are starting as early as September, per Nielsen.

“The market is still very active,” said Campbell. “Not only are [brands] starting way earlier to build out these larger programs, but they're also coming in as we get closer to the holiday season, and we're seeing total investments grow, as well.”

Brands flock to Disney for three reasons, Campbell said. The company is a trusted source for storytelling with titles that span generations, from “Home Alone” to “The Nightmare Before Christmas;” it delivers live cultural moments, including holiday TV specials, that serve as shared experiences; and it gives advertisers multiple ways to reach consumers across platforms.

Disney’s multichannel approach relies on a StorySync strategy that works with brands to develop seven key elements: sponsorships, in-show integrations, custom content, social and influencer, commerce, experiential and halo enhancements. The company is working to add new shoppable activations to the StorySync framework that can help drive brand revenue during the holiday period.

“We not only encourage people to participate and interact with these brands, but also give them the levers in order to be able to transact with these brands through our shoppable moments,” Campbell said. “How do we add value for our consumers and our viewers, and how do we bring brands to be able to help us add that value?”

“Dancing with the Stars,” now in its 35th season, continues to be a durable property for Disney, with the competition show seeing an 11% episode-to-episode viewership increase en route to the season finale on Nov. 24, Campbell said. Sephora served as the seasonlong beauty sponsor for the show, partnering on social-first custom content and becoming the first show partner to incorporate Disney’s Shop the Stream solution.

The show will keep the action going with a holiday special on Dec. 1. Ocean Spray will have a sponsored dance, custom content, in-show mentions and lower-third graphics, while Christmas retailer Balsam Hill provided decorations and will appear in lower-third graphics.

“That franchise has expanded now,” Campbell said. “No matter the opportunity, we're seeing brands really want to attach themselves.”

In addition, Kraft Mac and Cheese is partnering with Freeform’s “25 Days of Christmas” as part of a long-term strategic partnership between Kraft Heinz and Disney that kicked off this summer. Capital One will join as the first presenting broadcast sponsor for the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony on Dec. 8, and Disney has secured new and returning sponsors for the “Wonderful World of Disney: Holiday Spectacular” and New Year’s Rockin’ Eve programming.

After the holiday season, Disney Advertising will continue apace, with the college football playoffs and national championship game, the Grammy and Oscar ceremonies and the Super Bowl, together making up what Disney ad chief Rita Ferro has described as the company’s “biggest year ever.” Next month, the company will begin introducing creator content to Disney+ through a partnership with TikTok that was announced earlier this year.

“We're very excited about some new content formats that streaming is going to be able to provide,” Campbell said, citing microdramas as a possibility.
